### Station Seven — God as speaker, and the meaning of speech and the Book

The chapters that follow explain speech and the Book, and the difference between them, as God has inspired me to do.

#### Chapter 1 — What it means to speak

Know that speaking is an inward power of the speaker[^V07-1] that has an effect.[^V07-2] The word comes from a root meaning “to wound,” and speaking serves to make something known and bring it to light.[^V07-3] So when someone says that speech is an attribute of the speaker, they mean the quality of speaking itself.[^V07-4] When they say that speech exists in the speaker, they mean that speaking is something the speaker does, not an added quality that simply resides in the speaker.[^V07-5] And when they define a speaker as one who brings speech into being, they mean ordinary speech: it happens within the speaker when the mouth moves, not when it is still.[^V07-6] It is the breath that comes out from within the speaker as he speaks. It is not something separate from him, as a book is separate from its writer or an image from its maker. If it were separate, it would be writing or depiction, not speaking and explaining.

One of the knowers said: “The first word to open the ears of possible beings was ‘Be!’”—a word that brings existence.[^V07-7] The world appeared through speech; indeed, the whole world is made up of different kinds of speech,[^V07-8] arranged in twenty-eight levels within the Breath of the All-Merciful.[^V07-9] This Breath is the outpouring of existence from the source of generosity and mercy. Possible beings are stages in the way this outpouring takes shape. Beings that exist through intellect rather than physical matter are lofty letters, God’s complete words that never pass away or diminish.[^V07-10] Bodily substances are like combinations of nouns and verbs: they can be broken apart and decay. Their lasting qualities and passing features are like the structure and endings of words. All of them are sustained by the breath of existence, which these thinkers call “the created Reality through which things come to be.” In the same way, letters and words exist through a human being’s inner breath. The human being was created in the image of the All-Merciful, with different levels and points of articulation.

#### Chapter — The purpose of speech

When a speaker decides to speak, his first aim is to form the letters and words and bring them out from within himself through the organs of speech. This is the act of making something known. Any result that follows from a command, a prohibition, a statement, a wish, a call, a question, or another kind of speech is a second aim, separate from that act of making something known.

The two aims differ only in some kinds of speech. Speech falls into three broad kinds: highest, middle, and lowest. In the highest kind, speech itself is the first and main aim. Nothing more important follows it, because it is the end toward which everything else is directed. This is like God bringing the world of command into being by the word “Be!” alone. These are God’s complete words and intellectual realities, which never pass away or diminish. We will prove their existence later, if God wills. Their coming into being has no aim beyond God’s command itself. That is why the noble philosopher says in *Theology of Aristotle* that, for separate beings, what a thing is and why it exists are one: its very reality is its perfection and its end.

In the middle kind, speech has another purpose, but the result follows from it without fail. One example is God’s command to the angels in the heavens and the angels who govern the spheres and stars. He commands them to carry out their tasks: to order and move things, to long for what is good, and to worship God, all for other intellectual ends. They do not disobey God; they do what He commands. In this way they receive the benefit of the speech sent to them, as you will learn. The same applies to the angels who govern earthly nature and the elements: those who oversee mountains, seas, minerals, clouds, winds, and rain. When God’s command reaches them, either directly or through another command—not through the act of creating them—they obey and do not rebel. The Qur'an says, “They do what they are commanded,” rather than “They do what they are told to do.” This wording hints at this kind of speech.

In the lowest kind, speech has another purpose, but the result may or may not follow. Even when it does follow, it could fail if the one addressed were not protected from error and disobedience. This is like God’s commands and messages to those who are morally responsible: human beings and jinn. They receive them through angels and messengers. They live in a world of change and mixture, opposites and conflict, where things can break down. In this kind of speech—a command delivered through a messenger—some obey and some disobey. But a command given without an intermediary, or through another command, can only be obeyed.

The highest kind of speech is the creative command: “Our command is only one, like the blink of an eye.” It belongs to the world of God’s decisive decree. The middle kind is the forming command: “We created all things in due measure.” The lowest kind is the lawgiving command: “He has laid down for you the same faith that He entrusted to Noah.” These three kinds are also found in the perfect human being. As God’s representative, created in the image of the All-Merciful and guided by clear proof from his Lord, a perfect human being reflects all three kinds of speech. His nature brings together what is in the worlds of command and creation: he originates, forms and brings things into being, and moves his organs through the will.

The highest kind of speaking and hearing for such a person is his conversation with God. He receives knowledge and understanding from God through the language of his readiness; the conversation goes both ways.[^V07-11] He hears, with the inner hearing of the heart, God’s intellectual speech and sacred address: the true knowledge and divine teachings God gives him.[^V07-12] He then becomes a speaker after having been a listener. His inner being rises from the ability to think to active understanding, and then to the simple intellect, which can give the soul detailed knowledge from the storehouse of its own simple reality whenever it wills. Once it becomes this simple intellect, it speaks true knowledge and real understanding. Its only purpose is to give detailed form to hidden truths held together in a single whole, and to bring what lies hidden into the soul’s awareness and the tablet of imagination.

The middle kind is the soul’s command to its powers, organs, and tools. It acts through the soul’s inner powers, which move the body’s natural powers, just as the angels of the heavens move the angels of the earth. The soul’s rule reaches the powers and organs of the body by God’s permission. The servants of this small world are made to obey the spirit. Their places and bodies cannot resist it. When the soul tells the eye to open, it opens; when it tells the tongue to speak, it speaks; when it tells the foot to move, it moves; when it tells the hand to grasp, it grasps. The senses, powers, and organs all serve the human soul, much as angels and the great bodies of the heavens and earth serve God, having been made to obey and to serve.

The lowest kind is asking for something or calling for an action through the tongue or another organ. Here the aim is something beyond the words themselves, whether they are spoken, signaled, hinted at, or conveyed in another way. That aim may or may not be achieved because obstacles can arise. When these obstacles are absent, as in the first two kinds, the one addressed can only listen and obey. The failure of some of God’s commands that are delivered through intermediaries does not diminish His wisdom or power. His lawgiving commands, delivered to His servants through His messengers and the words of His revelation, are of this kind: some obey and some disobey.

#### Supporting testimony

The following words of the author of *The Meccan Revelations* support our account: “When God speaks to His servant in secret, without intermediaries, understanding accompanies His speech: what He speaks is what you understand, and it does not come later. If it comes later, it is not God’s speech. Whoever has not experienced this does not know how God speaks to His servants. But if He speaks through an outward veil, through a prophet or someone else He chooses, understanding may come with the words or may follow later. That is the difference between the two.”[^V07-13]

These words point to three kinds of speech: a command without an intermediary, a command through an inward veil, and a command through an outward veil. God points to these three in the verse: “It is not for any human being that God should speak to him except by revelation, from behind a veil, or by sending a messenger.” Direct revelation is the true, immediate kind of speech. There, speech itself is the first aim and end. The second kind comes through an inward veil. Its purpose is something else, but the speech itself is enough to bring it about because the result follows without fail. In both kinds, understanding cannot be separated from what is heard, whether it is the speech itself or something that must follow from it. Obedience follows too: either listening is itself obedience, or it necessarily leads to obedience.

The third kind is the lowest: speech that reaches people’s ears through angels and human messengers. Here, understanding may or may not come, and obedience may give way to disobedience, refusal, or acceptance. Understand this, my friend. It is a direct taste of what the people of God know. Do not imagine that the Prophet’s receiving God’s speech through Gabriel and hearing it from him is like your hearing the Prophet, or that the Prophet merely repeats what Gabriel says, as the community repeats the Prophet’s words. Far from it. These are two entirely different kinds of knowing and hearing.[^V07-14]

#### Chapter — The difference between speech and the Book, and between speaking and writing

Some careful thinkers say that God’s speech is different from His Book.[^V07-15] Speech is simple, while the Book is made of parts. Speech belongs to the world of command, while the Book belongs to the world of creation. Speech comes all at once, while the Book unfolds gradually. The world of command has no opposition, multiplicity, or change, as God says: “Our command is only one, like the blink of an eye, or even closer.” The world of creation, by contrast, contains many things, changes, and opposites, as God says: “There is nothing fresh or dry that is not in a clear Book.”

I say that speech and the Book are one reality, different only in how we consider them. You can see this in the human being, who is made in the image of his Lord—not equal to Him, but a sign of Him. When a person speaks or writes, his speech can also be called a book, and his book can also be called speech. Here is how.

When someone speaks and forms words, he creates sounds, letters, and words in the air that comes from within him, guided by an inward call from the soul. This is like the Breath of the All-Merciful and the outpouring of existence, which takes form as possible things so that God’s purposes, names, and highest attributes may appear in the shapes of what exists. These are the different stages through which absolute existence comes down, from the One and Hidden Reality, the source of all existence. We explained earlier in the First Journey how this outpouring of existence differs from limited, particular forms of existence.

The spoken forms and words have two relations: one to what brings them into being, and one to what receives and displays them. The first relation is necessary; the second is possible. In the first sense, they are speech. In the second, they are writing. When the forms of words exist in the mind’s tablet or on the sheet of air that comes from within, they are writing, because they are related to a receiver that can accept them. A receiver can only be ready to receive; it cannot create or bring something into being. So it needs a separate agent to bring the forms from possibility into reality. That agent is called a writer or artist, not a speaker. In our example, it is the rational soul. The inner breath or air serves as a simple tablet, and the letters and words are marks and images placed on it by the writer.

But when these forms are related to the soul[^V07-16] as acts to their agent, and as things that exist to what brings them into being, their relation is necessary, not merely possible. In this sense, they are speech, and the one described by them is a speaker. The speaker is the whole person—the soul, the air, and everything else that helps form these images and shapes. The whole person fits the definition of a speaker because he can form meanings and arrange letters and words without needing a separate maker who is different from him.

Once you see that the same spoken forms can be both speech and writing in different respects, and that the air of the breath can be both a writer and a speaker in different respects,[^V07-17] you can apply the same idea to everything beyond this bodily example, whether higher than it—the soul, the intellect, and God—or lower, like paper, a writing board, and earth. The soul, where intellectual forms and mental knowledge are impressed, is a writing tablet in one sense and a being that speaks and understands in another. It faces an intellectual maker and a higher pen that forms its ideas; it also faces a receiver that accepts those ideas and hears speech. The same applies at every level. So every book is speech in one respect, and every speech is a book in another. Take in what I have said, my friend, and see its worth. It is a subtle insight given to the author of these books, and it has many benefits that cannot all be counted here.

Among its benefits is that it can help settle a dispute among theologians about speech. The Muʿtazilites say a speaker is one who creates speech; the Ashʿarites say a speaker is one in whom speech exists. Both definitions are true from different points of view. This also helps explain how the world comes from God. Some say the world is related to God as a book is to its writer; others say it is related to Him as speech is to its speaker. Another group sees the relation as different from either of these.[^V07-18] This also reveals the secret of the world’s end and passing away, and the secret of the resurrection of spirits and the gathering of bodies, as you will see in the discussion of the afterlife, if God wills.

#### Chapter — How speech and the Book correspond

You may now see what I have been pointing out: the world of command, with everything in it, is best understood as a world of words, divine speech, and a complete order. The world of creation, with everything in it, is best understood as a world of actions and a detailed book that matches and reflects that complete order.

Here are some of the ways they correspond. God’s speech contains signs, as He says, “These are God’s signs, which We recite to you in truth.” The Book contains signs too: “These are the signs of the clear Book.” When speech takes a particular form and comes down into the created world, it becomes a book. In the same way, a command becomes an action when it comes down: “When He wills a thing, He only says to it, ‘Be!’ and it is.” The world of created things, which exists through the intellectual world, is God’s Book. Its verses are the beings and forms that appear in creation. God says: “In the alternation of night and day, and in what God created in the heavens and earth, are signs for people who are mindful of Him.”

These clear signs appear in the outer world, written on the pages of physical things. People of understanding can read them, reflect on their meaning, and notice how things resemble or differ from one another. In this way, they can recognize the intellectual signs written in the world of souls and intellects. They move from what they can sense to what they can understand, from the visible world to the hidden one, and from this life to the next. At last, they return to God, as He says: “We will show them Our signs on the horizons and within themselves until it becomes clear to them that this is the truth.”

One of the careful thinkers says that as long as a person is confined by the body and imprisoned in this world, bound by distance and place and by the chains of time and motion, he cannot see all the signs of the outer world and the inner self at once. He reads them one by one: word after word, letter after letter, day after day, hour after hour. He reads one sign, then another disappears from view. Events and states follow one another. It is like reading a scroll, one line after the next. This is because his sight and understanding are too limited to take in the whole at once. God says: “Remind them of the days of God. In this are signs for everyone who is patient and grateful.”

When a person’s inner sight grows stronger and is lit by guidance and help, as it will be at the Resurrection, he moves beyond the narrow, dark world of creation into the wide, bright world of command. Then he sees all the contents of this great Book at once: all the forms of beings and things.[^V07-19] It is like someone folding a scroll that contains many lines and words. God says: “On the Day We roll up the heaven as a scroll is rolled for books,” and, “The heavens will be folded in His right hand.” He says “in His right hand” because those on the left, the people of punishment, have no share in this folding of the heavens. For them, the heavens are never folded; their souls remain tied to places and veils, as God says: “They will have a bed of Hell, and coverings above them.”

#### Chapter — The beginning and end of speech and the Book

Speech and the Book, like all possible things, have a beginning and an end. Since the human being was created in the image of the All-Merciful, let us first look at how speech and writing begin in a person and return to him. This will help us understand the beginning and end of God’s speech and Book.

When someone decides to speak or write, the process begins with a simple, general idea in the rational soul. From this power, an effect appears in the rational soul as it thinks the idea through in detail. This is the inner heart. From it, another effect appears in the storehouse of imagination, the animal soul, which is the inner chest. The inner chest stands to the inner heart as the Footstool stands to the Throne.[^V07-20] The body’s pine-cone-shaped heart and rounded brain display the inner heart and chest, as the outermost heaven and the sphere of the fixed stars display the true Throne and Footstool.

Then the effect passes to the brain through the animal spirit, which this account describes as a fine, naturally warm bodily substance, roughly spherical in form. The effect that reaches it is the imagined form[^V07-21] of the speech or writing. From there, the form appears outside through the organs and limbs. Sound and letters take shape on the page of the air or on the page of paper. This is the furthest point in their descent: from the Throne of the heart, or from what is higher than it, down into the simple world of air or earth.

The effect then rises back. It reaches the ear, a cartilage organ, or the eye, an organ connected to the nerves. From either one it passes through muscles, tendons, and nerves into the veins, then through the spirits of the brain to the soul’s inner spirit. From there it reaches the storehouse of imagination and the soul’s powers, then the theoretical intellect and what lies above it, in an order from lower to higher and from less to more noble. This upward path reverses the downward one. The beginning of the first path is the end of the second, just as God says: “As We began the first creation, We will bring it back.”

Now use this example to understand the beginning of God’s speech and Book. Look at it with inner sight, for this is one of the wonders of the human being.[^V07-22]

Know that the realities of God’s signs, and the wonders of His wisdom, generosity, and mercy, are first held in God’s knowledge, in the deepest hiddenness, where no one knows them but Him. Then they are written in the Pen of His power in a simple form, free from multiplicity and detail. They are also written in the Preserved Tablet and in the intellects of the angels nearest to God. From there they take form in the souls of the angels who govern creation, then in the heavenly books and tablets that can be erased and rewritten, and then down to the lowest heaven. From there they descend to earth in stages, at the right times and for the right purposes.

This is like a speaker who first thinks about what he wants to say, forms it in his mind, and pictures it in his imagination. Then he brings it out from his inner world, from one level of hidden thought to the next, until it appears in the outer world where it can be seen and heard. God’s wisdom and His revealing what lies hidden in His knowledge, unseen keys, and stores of mercy likewise move from the hidden world to the visible one. They descend to the lowest point in the ordering of creation, as God says: “A sending down from the One who created the earth and the high heavens,” and, “He directs the affair from heaven to earth, then it rises back to Him.” That is when the return and ascent to Him begin. God says: “To Him good words rise, and He raises up good deeds.”[^V07-23]

“Everyone in the heavens and earth comes to the All-Merciful as a servant. He has counted them one by one, and each will come to Him alone on the Day of Resurrection.” This return to the All-Merciful happens only in human form, through the stages of human life. To understand why, consider the perfect human being and his levels of growth. When he reflects on the heavens and earth, their image enters his heart. If he closes his eyes, he can still see that image in his imagination, present to him even more fully than the outer image was present to his sight. From imagination the image passes to the soul. If the soul has reached active understanding, it carries the image to the simple intellect, united with the Active Intellect. There it grasps the realities of the things that first entered through the senses, then imagination and the world of images, and from there the world of command and intellect.

The copies of the world in our senses match the copies in those higher worlds, which in turn match the copy in the Preserved Tablet, written by the divine Pen. These books and copies are arranged in their first coming into being from the more noble to the less noble, and from what is nearer to God to what is farther away. What is in the Pen comes before what is in the Preserved Tablet; that comes before the Tablet of erasure and confirmation; and that comes before the pages of the world, written in the ink of bodily matter.

Their order in returning to their origin is the reverse of their first order. First comes their existence in the senses and in bodily matter, then their imaginal existence, then their detailed intellectual existence, then their simple intellectual existence in the Pen, and finally God’s eternal knowledge. The process returns to where it began. God begins creation, then repeats it, and to Him you will return.[^V07-24]

This too is one of the subtle works of God’s wisdom: He creates the perfect human being, who grows from a small world into a great one. All existence is like a single person that has made a full circuit back to itself, or a great book whose first page is also its last. God began the book with the Intellect and ended it with the one who knows. He says: “Do they not see how God begins creation and then repeats it? That is easy for God. Say: ‘Travel through the earth and see how He began creation. Then God will bring about the later creation. God has power over all things.’”

#### Chapter — Why God sent down Books and sent messengers to creation

Know that when God willed to bring things into being and create the realities of every kind of thing, so that His names and attributes might appear, He held countless forms of knowledge that had not yet appeared, many words with no means of expression, and many books with no pages or paper. All this was before souls and the worlds around us existed.[^V07-25] He addressed with the command “Be!” what was held in His knowledge, when nothing else existed. The first things He brought into being were letters of the intellect and creative words. They stood on their own, without matter, and had their own movements and the powers needed to act. This was the unseen world of God’s first decree. Then He began to write the books: to shape the words, arrange the verses on the tablets of substances and dimensions, and form simple and composite things with the ink of matter. This was the world where creation took shape in detail. God says, “God created seven heavens, and of the earth the like of them.”[^V07-26] He also says, “Then He formed them as seven heavens in two days, and revealed to each heaven its command.”[^V07-27]

When He had completed the writing of all things, He asked us to study this book of wisdom, read its spoken and written signs, and reflect on them. He says, “Read whatever you can of the Qur'an,” “Read in the name of your Lord who created,” and, “Have they not looked at the kingdom of the heavens and the earth?” He also says that in the creation of the heavens and earth and the change between night and day are signs for people of understanding, who remember God standing, sitting, and lying down, and reflect on the creation of the heavens and earth: “Our Lord, You did not create this in vain.”

At the beginning, our minds and our sight were weak. God says, “The human being was created weak.” We could not take in the full extent of these signs and great words. Their letters were too vast, their words too lofty, and their farthest edges too distant for us to reach.

Some people who received inner vision have said that every letter of God’s speech on the Tablet is greater than Mount Qaf, and that even if all the angels joined together to move it, they could not.[^V07-28] So we pleaded with Him in the language of our need and readiness: “Our God, have mercy on our weakness. Do not let us lose hope in Your mercy. Show us a way to study Your books and words, to reach what pleases You and enter Your gardens.” In keeping with His care for all, His perfect wisdom, His vast mercy, and His boundless power, He was gentle with us. He gave us a brief copy of the secrets in His books and a small sample of the meanings in His complete words. He says, “And in yourselves—will you not see?” The selves meant here are those of the perfected: the prophets and messengers. Each of their souls is a complete word sent down from the Lord of all worlds, an inscribed Book witnessed by those brought near to God. It contains signs of the visible and the unseen worlds, and the secrets of God’s power and dominion.

From among them, God chose one all-embracing word, to whom He gave speech that gathers many meanings. He sent him to us as a noble messenger, a clear light, a wise Qur'an, a straight path, and a revelation from the Mighty, the Merciful. His being became a model through which creation could be saved from the Fire. His Book became a way out of the darkness of devils, and the Qur'an sent down with him freed people from the chains of attachment to the self and from the whispers of accursed Satan.

So open your inner sight, human being, to the light of the Qur'an’s wisdom. Look at how the All-Merciful’s beginning is joined to the final messenger, who guides us to the world of light and joy. God is one in His essence, the first of all that is first; His representative is one in essence, the last of all that is last. “As He began you, so you will return.” God is the Lord of heaven and earth. His representative is a mirror in which all things are seen and through which God appears with all His names. “Whoever knows himself knows his Lord.”[^V07-29] The Prophet has a greater claim on the believers than they have on themselves. So, traveler toward God, know the Prophet if you wish to know your Lord. God says, “Whoever obeys the Messenger has obeyed God.” The Prophet said, “Whoever has seen me has seen God.” Set your relation to him in order, poor one. He is the root of existence; believers follow him in the Praiseworthy Station. A believer’s relation to him is sound when it is a relation of following, like one mirror facing another that reflects the sun. The believer joins him in the light. Whoever God has not given light has no light. Understand this.

Whoever cleanses the heart of images of everything else, shakes off the dust of attachment, and polishes the eye of reason until the haze of habit and intrusive thoughts is gone; whoever lets the self die to itself, immerses the heart in the sea of God’s majesty and greatness, and is gathered to the Lord, remains through God’s own abiding. “Whoever belongs to God, God belongs to him.” When such a person returns to ordinary awareness after being lost to himself, he moves from the whole to its details, from completion to its parts, and from union to distinction and understanding. Because he has become firmly rooted in God’s oneness and stands where God’s command and creation meet, his authority takes effect, his requests are answered, and he is honored with the power to bring things into being. He speaks with the speech of the Lord of all worlds. That is God’s favor, given to whomever He wills. God’s favor is great.

#### Chapter — How speech descends and revelation comes from God through the angel to the Prophet’s heart and then to creation, as it emerges from the unseen into the visible

Know that the Qur'an we have among us is both God’s speech and His Book, unlike the other heavenly books sent down to the other messengers, peace be upon them all.[^V07-30] Those books are studied and copied; they are not God’s speech in the direct sense meant here, even though every book is speech in one respect. Our point is that the Qur'an is God’s own speech.[^V07-31] We also say that what was sent down to our Prophet is both Qur'an and Criterion, while the other heavenly books are a criterion alone.[^V07-32] The difference is like the difference between knowledge held in one undivided whole and knowledge unfolded in detail. As God’s speech, the Qur'an is one of His spiritual lights, sent down to the hearts of servants whom God loves—not only those who love Him. God says, “We made it a light by which We guide whom We will of Our servants,” “He sent down the Book to you in truth,” and, “We sent it down in truth, and in truth it came down.” As a Book, it is made of forms, marks, and written signs containing rulings. These came down bit by bit from heaven and were placed on the pages of the hearts of those who love God and the inner tablets of those who travel toward Him. Others can write them on their own pages and tablets, where every Muslim can read them, every speaker can recite them, and every person guided to act on them can follow their rulings. All can find guidance in them and act by what they contain. In this guidance, prophets and their communities stand on equal ground. God says, “He sent down the Torah and the Gospel before, as guidance for people,” and, “They have the Torah, in which is God’s judgment.”

The great and noble Qur'an also contains the great teachings of divine knowledge. The final Messenger and his honored family learned from them. God says, “He taught you what you did not know; God’s favor to you is great.” The Qur'an also contains God’s finest qualities, which the Messenger made his own. God says, “You are surely of a magnificent character.” As a report tells us, his character was the Qur'an.

With these points in mind, we can explain how speech and the Book descend, as we will discuss more fully in the section on prophecy. The reason speech is sent down and the Book revealed is this: when the human spirit is freed from the body and leaves the prison of its bodily form and natural home, it turns toward its Lord to witness His greatest signs. It becomes clean of the stain of wrongdoing, pleasure, desire, habitual whispers, and attachment. Then the light of knowledge and faith in God and His highest kingdom appears to it. When this light grows stronger and becomes firmly established, it becomes a sacred reality. Philosophers call it the Active Intellect; the prophetic law calls it the Holy Spirit.

With this bright light of understanding, the secrets of what is in heaven and earth shine within the spirit, and the realities of things become visible to it. This is like the way visible forms appear to the eye when no veil blocks the sight. Here, the veil is the influence of nature and the distractions of this lower world. By their original nature, hearts and spirits can receive the light of wisdom and faith, unless a darkness corrupts them, such as unbelief, or a veil blocks them, such as sin and its like. God says, “God has sealed their hearts, so they do not understand,” and, “No, what they have done has covered their hearts like rust.” When the soul turns away from nature’s calls and the darkness of desire—from preoccupation with appetite, anger, the senses, and imagination—and turns toward God and the highest angelic realm, it reaches the greatest happiness. The secret of the unseen world appears to it, the holiness of God is reflected in it, and it sees God’s greatest signs, as He says: “He truly saw some of the greatest signs of his Lord.”

When this spirit is holy and powerful, its connection to what is above it gives it great power to illuminate what is below. One concern does not distract it from another, and its higher side does not block its lower side. It can attend to both at once because it is firmly rooted where the visible and unseen worlds meet. Weak spirits are not like this: when they turn toward one side, the other disappears from view; when they focus on one sense, they forget the rest.

When this holy spirit turns toward God and receives divine knowledge—not through human teaching but directly from God—its influence reaches its powers. A form of what it witnessed through its holy spirit takes shape in its human spirit, then appears in the outer world. It becomes visible to the outward senses, especially sight and hearing, the noblest of them. The Prophet sees a person of great beauty and hears speech of great eloquence. The person is an angel sent down by God to carry His revelation; the speech is God’s speech; and the angel holds a tablet containing a Book, God’s Book. This appearance is not merely an image produced by the imagination, with no reality beyond the mind, as people unfamiliar with inner knowledge claim.[^V07-33] God protect us from this belief, which comes from not understanding how revelation descends. We will explain this fully in the discussion of prophecy. For now, let me offer one more point to light the heart.

My friend, know that an angel has its own true reality and also a subtler form through which it relates to what is below it, as the soul relates to the body. These are not ordinary bodies made of parts, subject to change and never lasting for even two moments, as you learned in the study of nature. The body linked to an angel is more like the body brought back in the next life. The angel’s true reality belongs to the unseen world of God’s command, decree, and speech; the form through which it relates to lower things belongs to the created world, where things take shape. From these forms come the angels of the Tablets.[^V07-34] The greatest of them is Israfil, who bears the Trumpet. We will explain the reality of the Trumpet in the discussion of the afterlife and the gathering of bodies. The angels of the Tablets receive divine speech and knowledge from the angels of the Pen and write them on tablets that hold their measured forms. On his Ascension, the Prophet met the highest rank of angels and saw the Holy Spirit while awake.

When the Prophet’s spirit joined their world, the world of divine revelation, he heard God’s speech: a true exchange in which realities were made known. This is the giving and receiving that takes place at the distance of two bows, or closer—the station of nearness, the seat of truth, and the source of revelation and inspiration. This is true speech, as we explained earlier. When the Prophet joined the highest angels, he also heard the sound of their pens and their words. Their speech was God’s speech, descending into the places where they knew it—their own beings and intellects—because they stood near to God. The Prophet himself reported that, on the Night Journey, he reached a place where he heard the sound of the angels’ pens.

Then, when he descended to the heavenly kingdom, the form he had understood and witnessed appeared on the tablet of his soul, among the heavenly tablets where things take their measured forms. Its effect then reached his outward senses. These senses would seem dazed or asleep, because—as we have seen—the holy spirit uses them to attend to both the higher and lower worlds. It does not use them for animal concerns, but to travel toward God. The senses follow it in knowing God and obeying Him.

So when God spoke to the Prophet without an external veil, either directly or through an angel, and revealed the unseen to him, the angelic and heavenly forms were impressed on the core of his prophetic soul. A form of the revelation then appeared to his inner sense. The outward sense was drawn upward, and an image appeared to it that was inseparable from its true meaning and spirit. This was not like a dream or an empty fantasy. The angel appeared in a perceptible form that the Prophet could bear. He saw an angel of God in a form different from its form in the unseen world of God’s command, because when something descends from that world, it takes on a defined, created form. He saw the angel in its created form and heard speech that could be heard, after receiving it as intellectual revelation; or he saw a written tablet in the angel’s hand. First, the person receiving revelation joined the angel through his rational spirit, received divine knowledge, saw the greatest signs of his Lord with the mind’s inner sight, and heard the speech of the Lord of all worlds through inner hearing.

Then, when he came down from this lofty divine station, the angel appeared in a form his senses could perceive. That form passed into his outward senses, then into the air, and so on. The same is true of the angel’s speech: he heard sounds and ordered letters that only he could hear. They had descended from the unseen to the visible and emerged from his inner being into his outward awareness, without any external cause. The angel, its speech, and its Book all passed from the Prophet’s hidden, inward world to his senses. This was not the angel moving through space from its own station.[^V07-35] Each being has a known station that it does not leave. Rather, the Prophet’s spirit emerged from the unseen mode of being into outward appearance. That is why he seemed dazed or faint. Then he saw and heard, and then he told others what had happened. This is what it means for the Book and speech to descend from the Lord of all worlds.

Know from this what is meant when people say that holy spirits speak with angels while awake, while prophetic spirits meet them in sleep.[^V07-36] We must understand how a prophet’s sleep differs from other people’s sleep, just as the waking of prophets differs from ours. Their sleep is like our waking. The Prophet said, “My eyes sleep, but my heart does not.” ʿAlī said, “People are asleep; when they die, they wake up.” Perhaps what I have said will help you see that everything the prophetic spirit sees and witnesses in the unseen belongs to the same realm of speech and the Book, the speaker and the writer. This is fixed and necessary, not a chance event. Reflect on it.

For this reason, many passages of the Qur'an mention the sending down of the Book and its verses together with the creation of the heavens and earth and all that is in them. In Sūrat Āl ʿImrān, God says that nothing in heaven or earth is hidden from Him; He shapes people in the wombs as He wills; and He sent down the Book, with clear verses that are its foundation and others that are less clear. The same sūrah says, “These are God’s signs, which We recite to you in truth,” that God does not will injustice for the worlds, that all in the heavens and earth belong to Him, and that all affairs return to God. It also says that the creation of the heavens and earth and the change between night and day are signs for people of understanding, who remember God in every posture and reflect on creation.

In Sūrat al-Anʿām, God says, “He is God in the heavens and on earth. He knows what you keep secret and what you make known, and He knows what you do.” It also says, “No sign from their Lord comes to them without their turning away from it.” In Sūrat Hūd, God speaks of “a Book whose verses are made firm and then explained by One who is wise and fully aware,” and says that all things are in a clear Book; the sūrah also says that He created the heavens and earth in six days. In Sūrat al-Raʿd, He says, “These are the signs of the Book,” and, “What has been sent down to you from your Lord is the truth, though most people do not believe.” It goes on to describe God raising the heavens without pillars that people can see, setting the sun and moon to run for an appointed time, directing all affairs, and explaining the signs so that people may be sure they will meet their Lord.

In Sūrat Ibrāhīm, He says, “Alif Lam Ra. A Book We sent down to you so that you may bring people out of darkness into light, by their Lord’s permission, to the path of the Mighty, the Praiseworthy—God, to whom belongs all that is in the heavens and earth.” In Sūrat Yūnus, He calls the verses “the signs of the wise Book,” then says that the Lord is God, who created the heavens and earth in six days, and that the change between night and day and all that He created in the heavens and earth are signs for people who are mindful of Him. In Sūrat Yūsuf, He says, “How many signs in the heavens and earth do they pass by, yet turn away from them?” In Sūrat Ṭā Hā, He says, “We did not send down the Qur'an to make you suffer. It is a reminder for those who are mindful of God, sent down by the One who created the earth and the highest heavens.”

In Sūrat al-Shūrā, He says, “This is how God, the Mighty, the Wise, reveals to you and to those before you. To Him belongs all that is in the heavens and earth; He is the Most High, the Great.” In Sūrat al-Zukhruf, He swears by the clear Book, says that He made it an Arabic Qur'an so people may understand, and says that it is with Him in the Mother Book, exalted and wise. The sūrah then says that if people are asked who created the heavens and earth, they will answer, “The Mighty, the Knowing.” In Sūrat al-Dukhān, He swears by the clear Book and says, “We sent it down on a blessed night,” then speaks of the Lord of the heavens and earth, if people are certain.

In Sūrat al-Jāthiyah, He says that the Book is sent down by God, the Mighty, the Wise, and that there are signs in the heavens and earth for those who believe. In people’s creation and in the creatures God spreads through the earth are signs for those who are sure; the change between night and day, the rain that brings dead earth to life, and the shifting winds are signs for people who reason. “These are God’s signs, which We recite to you in truth,” the sūrah says, before warning of painful punishment. It also says, “He has placed at your service all that is in the heavens and earth.” These are signs for people who reflect. This kind of service is complete only for the perfected human being, when he joins the unseen world of God’s command, which encompasses all things. In Sūrat al-Aḥqāf, God says that He created the heavens and earth and all that lies between them in truth and for an appointed time. In Sūrat al-Jumuʿah, He says that all in the heavens and earth glorifies God, the King, the Holy, the Mighty, the Wise, and that He sent a messenger among the unlettered, who had been in clear error. There are many other passages like these.

The secret is that the Qur'an and the revealed verses are the very same signs in different forms: intellectual signs of speech in one place, Books written on tablets in another, created things in another, and spoken words heard by the ear or marks written in copies and seen by the eye.[^V07-37] The reality is one, but it appears in many ways. People witness it differently, and it exists in many realms.

#### Chapter — Uncovering the Book and revealing the inner meaning and spirit of speech to those who understand

Know, poor one, that the Qur'an came down from God to creation through a thousand veils, because people’s hearts and inner sight are weak. If the letter bāʾ in “In the name of God” came down from the Tablet in all its greatness to the Throne, the Footstool would melt and vanish. How, then, could it descend to the lowest heaven? God points to this when He says, “If We had sent this Qur'an down upon a mountain, you would have seen it humbled and split apart in awe of God.” One of the great masters of inner vision said that every letter on the Tablet is greater than Mount Qaf.[^V07-38] This is the Tablet mentioned in the verse, “Rather, it is a glorious Qur'an, on a Preserved Tablet.” The letter qāf points to the verse “Qaf. By the glorious Qur'an.” Although the Qur'an is one reality, it has many levels and places through which it descends, and its names differ accordingly.[^V07-39] At one level it is called “glorious”: “Rather, it is a glorious Qur'an.” At another it is called “mighty”: “It is a mighty Book.” At another it is “exalted and wise”: “It is with Us in the Mother Book, exalted and wise.” At another it is “noble”: “It is a noble Qur'an in a protected Book, touched only by the purified.” At another it is “clear”: “There is nothing fresh or dry that is not in a clear Book.” At another it is “wise”: “Ya Sin. By the wise Qur'an.” It has countless other names. The outer ear cannot hear them. If you had inner hearing, in the world of true love, inward attraction, and divine love, you would be among those who hear its names and witness its forms.

The different forms of beings, their varied qualities, and their opposing states are great signs. They help us know the Qur'an’s inner meanings, the lights of its beauty, the brightness of its verses, the secrets of its words, and God’s beautiful names and highest attributes. As we have said, the Book of creation corresponds to God’s speech in its unseen, intellectual form. In that form, it holds everything together as one whole; the world of creation unfolds it in detail. God says, “This is how We explain the signs for people who reason,” and, “A Book whose verses were made firm and then explained by One who is wise and fully aware.” The forms of beings on earth, in the heavens, and between them—the world of creation—unfold what is held in the world of intellect, the unseen world of God’s command. In the same way, everything in the worlds of God’s command and creation is a detailed Book of the names and attributes held in the divine world.

God says, “God’s are the most beautiful names, so call on Him by them, and leave those who misuse His names.” This verse calls us to learn wisdom and God’s oneness, to understand the worlds of God’s command and creation, and to study the signs on the horizons and within ourselves. We learn the names of God—the powers behind the forms of different kinds of beings—and call on Him by them only by reflecting on what He has made and what He sustains throughout the heavens and earth. That is why the Qur'an so often tells us to reflect on created things and their states. This is the path of knowledge followed by the realized mystics and divine scholars. They say that the world’s many different forms are forms of God’s names, their shadows and images, and the places where the divine world appears. For whatever exists in any world, a higher and nobler form exists in the upper world of the divine names, in a simpler and holier way. What is with God is better and more lasting.

#### Chapter — Understanding the saying of the Commander of the Faithful, the leader of those who affirm God’s oneness: “All of the Qur'an is in the bāʾ of ‘In the name of God,’ and I am the dot beneath the bāʾ”

May God guide you, my friend. One of the spiritual stations reached by those who travel toward God and His kingdom through worship and certainty is this: they see, with their own eyes, the whole Qur'an—and indeed all the revealed books—in the single dot beneath the bāʾ of “In the name of God.” They may even see all beings gathered in that one dot.[^V07-40] In these books and elsewhere, we have shown by philosophical proof that “the simplest reality contains all things.” Aristotle, the leading teacher of the philosophical school that bears his name, says in the tenth treatise of his book known as *Theology of Aristotle*: “The One, pure, is the cause of all things. He is not like any of them. He is the beginning of things, but He is not the things themselves. All things are in Him.” He states this clearly in several places in his book.

Let me give you a sense of this through something familiar. When you say, “To God belongs all that is in the heavens and earth,” you have gathered all beings into one sentence. To describe them one by one would take many volumes. Now compare words with words and meanings with meanings: the world of meanings is far wider, and its kinds and individual things differ far more than words do. If someone were to leave this world as we experience it through the senses and imagination, and rise to a way of being guided by the intellect, entering God’s holy kingdom, he would witness the meaning of “God encompasses all things” and see himself contained and overcome by God’s greatness. He would then see his own being beneath the dot of the bāʾ of causation, under the One who causes all causes. He would witness the bāʾ in “In the name of God” and see its greatness, its majesty, the high rank of its meaning, and the secret it holds.[^V07-41]

How far we are from this! People like us see nothing of the Qur'an but black ink. We live in a world of darkness, where the ink stretches across a page: matter, bodies, dimensions, and the material stuff that underlies opposing things and numbers. A person can understand only what his power to understand allows; that power is always of the same kind as what it understands. The senses grasp only what can be sensed; imagination grasps only what can be pictured; the intellect grasps only what can be understood. Light is seen only by light. Whoever God has not given light has no light.

With the darkness of our eyes, we see only the dark letters of speech and the ink of the Book. If we leave this imagined existence and this unjust town, migrating toward God and His Messenger; if we cross the stages between us and our goal, die to the forms and states of this world—whether sensed, imagined, guessed, or understood—and turn away from them all, then our own existence is erased in the existence of God’s speech. If God then brings us to life after that death, moving us from erasure to wakefulness, from passing away to abiding, and from death to a lasting life that endures through God, we will see no blackness in the Qur'an. We will see only pure white, undiluted light, and certainty untouched by doubt. Then we will know the truth of God’s words: “We made it a light by which We guide whom We will of Our servants,” and, “We taught him knowledge from Us.”

At that point, we read the verses from the original copy: the Clear Imam, the Wise Remembrance, and the one who has knowledge of the Book—ʿAlī, the Commander of the Faithful. God says, “It is with Us in the Mother Book, exalted and wise.” That is why ʿAlī said, “I am the dot beneath the bāʾ,” and, pointing to his noble chest, “Here is great knowledge, if only I could find people able to carry it.” Perhaps this discussion has gone beyond what the mind can grasp and beyond the usual way of teaching and debate. But, as the saying goes, one word leads to another. My friends who know the truth will excuse me: love of the beloved moves me to count his fine qualities and beauty, and to speak of his being and attributes, his speech and Book, his Messenger, commands and prohibitions, and all his other works. That is what we are discussing, God willing. Let us return to our subject.

#### Chapter — The difference between God’s Book that is created and His Book that creates

This, too, is knowledge gained through experience. Only someone with inner sight can accept it: someone who knows the difference between an image sensed through the outward senses that begins outside them, and an image sensed through those same senses that begins within. The senses perceive both kinds but cannot tell them apart. When the power of the inner world rises and appears outwardly, some people may see with the eye of imagination something that is perceived without the outward senses. God says, “Hell is brought into view for those who see.” For such a person, the unseen becomes visible. Reports of this kind are widespread and the events have happened often. Among the reports connected with this verse is that the Messenger of God once came out holding two folded books, one in each hand. He asked his companions, “Do you know what these two books are?” He told them that the book in his right hand contained the names of the people of Paradise, along with the names of their fathers, tribes, and clans, from the first person God created until the Day of Resurrection. The book in his left hand contained the names of the people of Hell and the names of their fathers, tribes, and clans, from the first person God created until the Day of Resurrection.

From this report, together with what we have already said about how revelation and the Book descend to the prophets, we can understand the difference between God’s Book and a created book. If a created being tried to write all those names as they are in the two books, it could not. The task is beyond it, and all the paper in the world would not be enough. The same is true of *al-Jafr* and *al-Jāmiʿah*, the books held by the purified Imams of the Prophet’s family, peace be upon them all. They inherited them from one generation to the next until the coming of the Mahdi. They contain everything lawful and unlawful, everything that will happen until the Day of Resurrection, and everything people need—even the payment due for a scratch.

Another story tells of a simple-minded pilgrim from the people of Mecca. During the farewell circumambulation of the Kaʿba, a man joked with him, “Have you received your release from the Fire?” The pilgrim asked, “Have other people received theirs?” “Yes,” the man replied. The pilgrim began to weep. He entered the Hijr, clung to the curtains of the Kaʿba, and begged God to give him a document releasing him from the Fire. His friends and the people around him scolded him and explained that the man had been joking, but he did not believe them and continued to pray. As he did, a sheet of paper fell from above, near the water spout. It said that he was released from the Fire. He was overjoyed and showed it to everyone. The sign of that document was that it could be read the same way from every side. Whenever the paper was turned, the writing turned with it. People knew it was a sign from God. There are many stories and reports of this kind; listing them all would take too long.

Someone may object: If the difference between God’s creating Book and a created book is what I have described—the first appearing from the inner, unseen world in the outer, visible world, and being perceived first by imagination and then by the senses, while the second is perceived first by the senses and then by imagination—then only people whose inner lives are ruled by the next world should be able to see God’s Book. Other people, including those veiled from the inner world, should not see it.

I answer: perhaps other people see it by following along with that person, because his soul affects them and his state spreads to them through hidden causes whose details they do not know. It is also possible that the people present are so absorbed in amazement at what is happening and in thought about it that their outward senses stop taking in outside things. Their attention then returns inward, to imagination and the unseen world.

#### Chapter — The Prophet’s saying that the Qur'an has an outer meaning, an inner meaning, a limit, and a point of arrival

Know[^V07-42] that, like a human being, the Qur'an has an outward side and an inward side. Each has its own outer and inner levels, and its inward meaning opens into deeper levels until God knows its full interpretation. Only God knows its deepest meaning. A report says that the Qur'an has an outer meaning and an inner meaning, and that the inner meaning has another inner meaning, through seven layers. These correspond to the inner levels of a person: nature, soul, breast,[^V07-43] heart, intellect, spirit, secret, and hidden self.

The Qur'an’s outward side is the copy we can touch and see, with its written marks. Its inward side is what the inner senses perceive and readers and reciters retain in their store of impressions, such as images in the imagination. The inner senses do not perceive a meaning by itself. They perceive it mixed with bodily associations and features of size and shape. Yet they can retain it after the thing first sensed is no longer present. These are the two worldly levels of the Qur'an, open to every human being. Its inner reality and secret belong to the next world. Each of these has many ranks, degrees, and stations.

The first of these inner levels is reached by someone able to understand meanings in their full truth, free of features that do not belong to what they are and of the effects of physical matter. These meanings come from the first principles of the intellect. In them, many things meet as one; conflict and opposition fall away, and separate things—even opposites—come together. This is unlike the world of matter and bodies.

The human spirit cannot grasp such a shared meaning unless it rises from the world of creation to the unseen world of God’s command, and shakes the dust of the body’s grave and its senses from itself. A mind buried in matter cannot understand what lies beyond it, just as something known only through the senses cannot itself understand.

You have learned that every kind of perception—sensing, imagining, supposing, or reasoning—requires some freedom from matter. The ability to perceive depends on a degree of separation from matter and from this world. These degrees differ greatly, as do our chances of reaching the truths of things, drawing near to God, and receiving from Him.

At one time the human spirit receives knowledge through the senses, when it descends to the level of the body and its senses. At another, it receives images through the imagination. It may also receive knowledge through the intellect, from the unseen world of God’s command. And sometimes it receives divine knowledge from God directly, with no veil of sense or intellect. The senses act in the world of creation and measure; the intellect acts in the world of command and governance. What is beyond both creation and command is hidden from the senses and the intellect. The light of truth is perceived only by God’s light, and reached only through the power of the One who holds command and creation. As the report from Abū ʿAbd Allāh tells us, the Commander of the Faithful said: “Know God through God,[^V07-44] the Messenger through the message,[^V07-45] and those entrusted with authority through their command to do what is right.”[^V07-46]

To show that the Qur'an has different ranks, God describes it this way: “It is a noble Qur'an in a protected Book, touched only by the purified, sent down from the Lord of all worlds.” These descriptions point to its different levels and stations. The highest is its nobility with God; the lowest is its descent into this world from the Lord of all worlds.

God’s speech, as His speech, has a lofty divine station before it descends into the unseen world of command, the Preserved Tablet; before it descends to the lowest heaven, the Tablet of Erasure and Confirmation; and before it descends into the world of creation. No one knows or perceives this highest station but God. Even the prophets perceive it only in the station of oneness, when they have shed the limits of possibility, left behind both created worlds, and passed beyond both the world of command and the world of creation. They reach the distance of two bows, or closer, as the greatest of the prophets and the best of humankind, the Prophet Muhammad, described his state: “I have a time with God when no angel brought near or messenger sent can be with me.”

This is what is meant by the verses, “No one knows its interpretation except God and those firmly grounded in knowledge,” and, “No one in the heavens and earth knows the unseen except God.” The verse “Is one whose heart God has opened to Islam, so that he walks in a light from his Lord” points to the level of the heart and inner sense. The verse “In this there is a reminder for whoever has a heart, or listens with full attention” points to it too. So does the verse quoting those who deny the truth: “If only we had reasoned or listened, we would not be among the people of the Blaze.”

The verse “Give him protection until he hears the speech of God” points to the outward senses, one of the Qur'an’s levels. To show that people of knowledge have different ranks, God says, “We raise in rank whomever We will,” and, “Above every person of knowledge is one more knowing.” He says, “We raised some messengers above others,” and, of the angels, “Each of us has a known station.” These are examples of the different ranks and levels of the Qur'an, just as there are different ranks and levels in a human being.

The Qur'an’s lowest level is the copy between its covers, just as a person’s lowest level is the skin and outer surface. At every level, the Qur'an has bearers who preserve and write it. They may touch it only when they are clean of impurity—or, at the higher levels, free from the limits of created existence—and have left behind their place or their possibility.[^V07-47] A person concerned only with outer things understands only the Qur'an’s outer meanings: its wording, rhetorical features, practical rulings, and laws. Only people with understanding and insight can grasp the Qur'an’s spirit, secret, and inner core.

True wisdom comes only as God’s gift. A person becomes wise only when God gives that person wisdom and knowledge from Himself. Knowledge and wisdom are qualities of perfection; “the Knowing” and “the Wise” are among God’s most beautiful names. So anyone who receives wisdom must receive it through God’s gift. That is why, after saying, “He teaches them the Book and wisdom,” God says, “This is God’s favor. He gives it to whomever He wills, and God’s favor is great.” He calls wisdom “a great good,” saying, “Whoever is given wisdom has been given a great good. Only people of understanding take heed.”

#### Chapter — Clarifying that the Book’s inner core is known only to God’s people, those with insight and understanding

Brothers, you who travel the path and care about your faith and the strange meanings in God’s clear speech: no one, however sharp-minded, can understand its mysteries, signs, and wonders unless they have studied the knowledge of certainty in the school of the family of Yā Sīn, the household of the Prophet, and in the school of the holy ones and people of remembrance. They must learn to read the signs written on the great Tablet and its original, noble copy, the Clear Guide. Their teacher must be the One who taught you what you did not know, for God’s favor to you is great. Their guide must be the one who said, “My Lord taught me good manners and taught me well.” The teacher who writes their lessons with the Pen and shapes their pages with knowledge and wisdom is your most generous Lord, who taught by the Pen and taught the human being what he did not know.

God alone is their teacher, not any other cause—not reasoning, imitation, analogy, reports, or listening. They receive the Qur'an from the One who is wise and knowing, or hear it with an inner hearing in the unseen world from the One who is obeyed and trustworthy.

A Qur'anic sign and a hint about God’s speech: the first things revealed to the children of the Holy Spirit in the school of holiness are the meaning of the Tablet, the Pen, writing, and written marks; the meaning of Alif Lām Mīm, Ṭā Hā, Yā Sīn, and the wise Qur'an; the meaning of Ṣād and the Qur'an full of remembrance, Qāf and the glorious Qur'an, and Nūn, the Pen and what it writes. They also learn the meaning of the separate letters at the beginning of some Qur'anic chapters, and of God’s complete words, first the single letters and then the words formed from them.

When God’s care turns to the growth of these high spirits, He feeds them with milk from the breasts of the unseen kingdom and the gardens of Paradise. He gives them fine spiritual food from His mercy and favor, wrapped in single letters and given as symbols and signs of good news, so outsiders will not see what is meant.[^V07-48]

For those who are not ready to reach the world of secrets and the source of lights, God first writes condensed letters on the tablets of their spirits. Perhaps they will remember, follow the craft of their forebears, write as they wrote, rise to their own stations, find guidance in God’s signs, and return to their Lord. “We were once letters on high,[^V07-49] then we came down[^V07-50] into lines below.”[^V07-51]

In the world of the secret and the hidden, these separate letters are called “letters in condensed form” or “letters that hold a whole.” There, the letters joined together[^V07-52] become separate, and the separate letters become joined. This is the Day of separation and distinction, when God separates what is good from what is bad. Yet it is also, in another sense, the Day of gathering: “This is the Day of Decision. We have gathered you and those who came before,” and, “for a Day about which there is no doubt.” The people of this world see separate letters joined together, joined letters separated, and a single letter as many letters in meaning. The first sign of rising from this level is to learn the meaning of the separate letters[^V07-53] and how they descend[^V07-54] to the Tablet of the Book, then to the open hearts of people of understanding. God points to this when He says, “We have made the word clear to them so that they may remember.” He also says of another people, “We have made the signs clear for people who know,” and, “A Book whose verses have been made clear.”

You who are traveling the path, it is now clear that the first thing written on the tablet of a beginner’s heart is the letters of the alphabet. This prepares the reader to recite the sacred page and obey God’s command: “Read in the name of your Lord who created,” and, “Read whatever you can of the Qur'an.” Then recitation and remembrance become easier, and reading becomes possible: “We have made the Qur'an easy to remember. Is there anyone who will take heed?” God guards the reader’s heart from Satan’s whispers, as He says, “We will surely guard it.” The heart becomes a protected heaven, safe from the attacks of rebellious jinn and devils. It is adorned with the stars of the clear Book, which drive away the doubts of those who deny God, the errors planted by whisperers, and the claims of those who pretend to know. God says, “We have guarded it from every accursed devil, except one who steals a hearing, then is pursued by a clear flame.”

Here is a warning and a reminder: whoever has not yet felt the power of the next world or risen from the grave of this life cannot know the meanings, signs, letters, and words of the Qur'an. They have not met its separate letters, nor seen the face of the One who speaks it and brings it into being, or the greatness of the One who wrote it.

Wake up, you who are deceived. Rise from your bed, you who are held down, and set out on God’s path, migrating to God and His Messenger, to witness the highest kingdom and hear His greatest signs. Do not sit with people who waste their lives and neglect what matters. Leave those who make their faith a joke and use their lives for play; worldly life has deceived them. That is the full extent of their knowledge. God rebukes people like them: “What is wrong with these people? They can hardly understand a word.” The Messenger complained to God about them: “My Lord, my people have abandoned this Qur'an.”

A man may be learned, clever, sensible, eloquent, and skilled in language, grammar, rhetoric, and debate. He may know how to defeat his opponents in religious argument. Yet he may never have heard a single letter of the Qur'an or understood a single word of the Book God sent down to His servant. He may have turned away from what is truly knowledge, light, understanding, and wisdom because he is absorbed in what most people call knowledge, faith, understanding, and certainty.

So leave the house that hides you from the truth and step away from its doorway. Take off the clothes of ignorance. Break free from the habits and rules you follow without thought. Then you may see God’s wonders and greatness in the descent of the Qur'an and the Book and Criterion. You may see the doors to the treasures of heaven and earth opened by the keys of knowledge of the unseen, a knowledge free from doubt. If death catches you as you leave your first home and travel toward a new nature and the next life, your reward will be with God. God will be your protector and will reward you, as He says, “Whoever leaves his home, migrating to God and His Messenger, and then dies, has earned his reward from God.”

#### Chapter — Describing the Qur'an through symbols and signs

Know[^V07-55], you who reflect and think deeply, that when the Qur'an lifts the veil of greatness from its face and draws back the covering of majesty and glory from its inner secret, it heals everyone sick with ignorance, quenches the thirst of those seeking life and truth, and cures hearts diseased by bad character and deadly ignorance.

The Messenger of God said that the Qur'an is a remedy and a treasure: nothing is richer than it, and nothing can replace it. The Qur'an is God’s strong rope, sent down into this world to free people bound by attachments and burdens: love of family and children, status and wealth, the desire for food and sex, gold and silver, horses, and endless hopes.

Though the Qur'an is so great in rank, home, secret, and meaning, it has taken the form of letters and sounds, words and phrases. This is God’s mercy and kindness toward His creation: He brings it close to their understanding and meets them where they are. Otherwise, what comparison is there between dust and the Lord of lords? In each of its letters are a thousand symbols and hints, a thousand graces and attractions that draw the hearts of lovers longing for union.

A call came from heaven to free the prisoners from the dungeon of this world: “Remind them, for the reminder benefits those who believe.” The Qur'an’s letters and sounds were spread like a net, baited with meanings to catch the birds of heaven. Each bird has its own food, known to the One who brought creatures into being and will bring them back. The true aim is to catch a certain kind of heavenly bird with a particular heavenly food. Whoever feeds on it understands the speech of all birds. This is why the net was spread over the earth. Others are like those of whom God says, “It is the same to them whether you warn them or not: they will not believe.”

Some people could not bear to look at this truth, the food that gives strength to hearts and spirits. So they were told not to enter waters too deep for them: “Keep them back, for they cannot bear it. Stop them; they were not created for this.” God is not questioned about what He does, but they will be questioned. What can the blind know of the true colors?

Those whose hearts have become lamps filled with light from the Qur'an can grasp the secrets of its ideas, words, and verses. They are told: “Show the manners God and His Messenger taught you. Keep quiet, and follow the pace of the weakest among you. Do not uncover the sun before the eyes of bats and cause them to perish.”

Come down from your high station to the lowest heaven, so those with weak sight may feel at ease with you and take what they can from the light that shines from behind the veils between you and them. Be as the poet said: “We drank, then poured our extra drink onto the earth; the earth, too, should share in the noble cup.”

That is why the Qur'an contains something useful for everyone. Each person receives a share of its spiritual and worldly gifts. “It is provision for you and your livestock,” and, “There is nothing fresh or dry that is not in a clear Book.” It contains truths and fine gifts that nourish the heart and spirit. It also contains detailed knowledge and practical help: stories, laws, inheritance, blood-money, marriage, and other matters useful to people at every level. So the Qur'an holds spiritual and worldly food, gifts for this life and the next. Nothing is left out. If you had a way into the inner kingdom of the Qur'an, you would see that it explains everything.

#### A warning and reminder

Know that Qur'anic calls such as “O human being” and “O you who believe” are addressed to God’s beloved, those who live by divine wisdom and are close to Him—not to those who turn away, deceive themselves, and deny the truth. The latter receive only the outer shell of its words and phrases. “They are kept from hearing,” and, “If God had known any good in them, He would have made them hear. Even if He had, they would have turned away.”

This has been true from the beginning, and it remains true. My beloved, if God had not judged that there was good in you and made this difficult path easy for you, you would follow others like a blind person—if you are among those who submit and not those who reject the truth. If you are ready for this path and God has given you a sound eye, not covered by blind following or damaged by prejudice, then when you open your eye, you will see what lies before you. You will not need a guide to lead you.

A person who follows others without seeing is like a blind walker who needs a guide. But not every path a guide can take can be followed by a blind person. Following another person works only for simple tasks and low-level actions. A blind person can be led only so far. When the path narrows until it is sharper than a sword and finer than a hair, a bird may fly over it, but a blind person cannot follow. If the way becomes as subtle as water, and can only be crossed by swimming, a skilled swimmer may cross it alone but may not be able to take another person along. If a guide were like a bird flying through the sky of the highest kingdom, or a skilled swimmer crossing the seas of truth and meaning, a blind person could not imitate the bird’s flight or the swimmer’s journey.

The knowledge in God’s speech and Book is far beyond what most people can grasp by careful thought, as walking on land is far easier than walking on water. People can learn to walk on land, but neither walking on water nor flying through the air can be learned by imitation or instruction. It takes the strength of certainty.

When someone told the Prophet that Jesus could walk on water, he replied, “If his certainty had grown stronger, he would have walked through the air.” The people of the Qur'an, who are God’s own people, have eyes with which to see God’s signs, ears with which to hear His words, and hearts with which to understand the secrets of His wisdom and law. They have hands with which to receive the gifts of His mercy and feet with which to walk in His garden of honor and generous care.

#### Chapter — The books that record what is erased and what is made firm

The great scholar Muḥyī al-Dīn ibn ʿArabī said in the 316th chapter of his book *The Meccan Revelations* that the Prophet, speaking of himself with pride, said that he had been taken on the Night Journey until he reached a place where he could hear the scratch of pens. This refers to God’s words, “To show him some of Our signs. He is the All-Hearing, the All-Seeing.” The pronoun “he” refers to Muhammad. He was taken on the Night Journey, saw the signs, and heard the pens. Since the word used means a scratching sound, he heard them as sounds.

This shows that a part of the world beyond the senses remained open to the Prophet, even beyond what he could see with his body. He reached it through hearing.[^V07-56] He heard the pens writing God’s decisions about what comes to be in the world. These pens are below the rank of the Highest Pen[^V07-57] and the Preserved Tablet. What the Highest Pen writes does not change; the Tablet is called “preserved” because nothing written there can be erased. These lower pens write on the Tablet of Erasure and Confirmation. God says, “God erases what He wills and confirms what He wills.” From these tablets, the laws and books are sent down to the messengers. That is why a law may be replaced by another, and a ruling within one law may later end.[^V07-58] This does not mean that God changes His mind.

Ibn Arabi also says that the phrase “Then He set a term, and another term appointed by Him” points to this writing. The same unseen reality is called “hesitation” when it is described as belonging to God: God says that He hesitates over nothing as He does over taking the soul of a faithful servant, even though He has already decreed that it will die. From this divine reality, he says, comes the changing and hesitation we find in the created world.[^V07-59]

He continues: These pens are lower in rank than the Highest Pen. The angel in charge of erasure is honored by God; it erases only as God commands. The writing and erasure of these pens belong to a divine quality that scripture describes as God’s “hesitation” when He reveals His message to His Messenger. Without this divine reality,[^V07-60] no two states in the world would differ, no one would hesitate or be uncertain, and everything would be fixed by an unchangeable decree. Yet the hesitation people feel is itself fixed within them by decree, because the world is held together by these realities.[^V07-61]

Ibn Arabi says: I have told you where these pens stand in divine knowledge, what gives them their power, what divine reality they depend on, and how they affect the heavens—the angels, stars, and spheres—and the elements and living things that arise from them. This is a remarkable unveiling of hidden truths. The effects of these pens are always at work in the world. They must write and establish the scattering of the stars, the breaking apart of the heavenly bodies, and the destruction of this earthly home.
